Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- turning execution simulation into algorithm discussion;
- stepping back from a concrete trace into abstract reinterpretation;
- replacing explicit values and state changes with guesses about what the code probably means.

loguru/_colorizer.py:

```
import re
from collections import namedtuple

Tag = namedtuple("Tag", ["name", "value"])
Token = namedtuple("Token", ["type", "value", "ansi"])

class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m", ...
        })
```

```
    def __init__(self):
```

```
def feed(self, text):
    self._text.append(text)
    while True:
        match = self._tag_pattern.search("".join(self._text))
        if not match:
            break
        start, end = match.span()
        tag = match.group(1)
        if tag.startswith("/"):
            name = tag[1:]
            self._close_tag(name)
        else:
            self._open_tag(tag)
        self._text = [self._text[0][end:]]?
```

```
import re
from collections import namedtuple

Tag = namedtuple("Tag", "name value")
Token = namedtuple("Token", "type value ansi")

class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})

            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
```

```
def done(self, strict=True):
    if self._text:
        self._tokens.append(Token("text", "".join(self._text), None))
        self._text = []
    if strict and self._stack:
        raise ValueError("closing tag not found")
    return self._tokens
```

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi[ansi_level])
    return "".join(result)
```

I recall a file `loguru/_colorizer.py`:

```
import re
from collections import namedtuple

Tag = namedtuple("Tag", ["name", "value"])
Token = namedtuple("Token", ["type", "value", "ansi"])


class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30,
            "red": 31,
            "green": 32,
            "yellow": 33,
            "blue": 34,
            "magenta": 35,
            "cyan": 36,
            "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90,
            "light-red": 91,
            "light-green": 92,
            "light-yellow": 93,
            "light-blue": 94,
            "light-magenta": 95,
            "light-cyan": 96,
            "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1,
            "dim": 2,
            "underline": 4,
            "blink": 5,
            "reverse": 7,
            "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update(
            {
                "normal": "\x1b[0m",
                "reset": "\x1b[0m",
                "clear": "\x1b[0m",
                "lred": "\x1b[91m",
                "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
                "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
                "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
                "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
                "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
                "lwhite": "\x1b[97m",
            }
        )
```

Let's recall `feed` method:

```
def feed(self, text):
    self._text.append(text)
    while True:
        match = self._tag_pattern.search("".join(self._text))
        if not match:
            break
        start, end = match.span()
        tag = match.group(1)
        if tag.startswith("/"):
            self._close_tag(tag[1:])
        else:
            self._open_tag(tag)
        self._text = [self._text[0][end:]]
```

Actually need handle text before tag. Let's reconstruct:

```
def feed(self, text):
    self._text.append(text)
    text = "".join(self._text)
    self._text = []
    pos = 0
    for match in self._tag_pattern.finditer(text):
        self._tokens.append(Token("text", text[pos:match.start()], None))
        tag = match.group(1)
        if tag.startswith("/"):
            self._close_tag(tag[1:])
        else:
            self._open_tag(tag)
        pos = match.end()
    self._text.append(text[pos:])
```

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}

            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",

            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
```

```
def _open_tag(self, tag):
    if tag in self._tags:
        ansi = self._tags[tag]
        self._stack.append(ansi)
        self._tokens.append(Token("tag", tag, ansi))
    else:
        match = re.match(r"(fg|bg) (.+)", tag)
        if match:
            type_, color = match.groups()
            if type_ == "fg":
                ansi = self._fg(color)
            else:
                ansi = self._bg(color)
            self._stack.append(ansi)
            self._tokens.append(Token("tag", tag, ansi))
        else:
            raise ValueError("unknown tag: <%s>" % tag)
```

This would restore previous state. For simple red, previous state empty -> `\x1b[0m`. For nested bold inside red, closing bold would restore red -> `\x1b[31m`. That seems plausible. Closing bold: previous state is red -> `\x1b[31m`. Closing red: previous state empty -> `\x1b[0m`. Output: `\x1b[31m\x1b[1mFoo\x1b[31m\x1b[0m`. That works because after bold, red code still active? Since bold remains active, output `\x1b[31m` does not turn off bold. So the text after closing bold would still be bold red, not just red. To restore red without bold, you need `\x1b[0m\x1b[31m`. So the previous state's ansi code alone is insufficient if it doesn't reset. Therefore loguru must output reset + previous state. Let's verify.

```
def _close_tag(self, tag):
    if tag not in self._stack:
        raise ValueError("closing tag not found")
    self._stack.remove(tag)
    if self._stack:
        ansi = "\x1b[0m" + "".join(self._tags[t] for t in self._stack)
    else:
        ansi = "\x1b[0m"
    self._tokens.append(Token("tag", "/" + tag, ansi))
```

Yes! That rings a bell. The stack contains tag names, and closing tag generates reset + all active tags' ANSI codes. This is common.

```
import re
from collections import namedtuple

Tag = namedtuple("Tag", ["name", "value"])
Token = namedtuple("Token", ["type", "value", "ansi"])

class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
            "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
            "lwhite": "\x1b[97m",
        })
        self._tags.update({
            "fg": "\x1b[38;5;%dm",
            "bg": "\x1b[48;5;%dm",
        })
```

```
def _open_tag(self, tag):
    if tag in self._tags:
        ansi = self._tags[tag]
        self._stack.append(tag)
        self._tokens.append(Token("tag", tag, ansi))
    else:
        match = re.match(r"(fg|bg) (.+)", tag)
        if match:
            kind, color = match.groups()
            if kind == "fg":
                ansi = self._fg_color(color)
            else:
                ansi = self._bg_color(color)
            self._stack.append(tag)
            self._tokens.append(Token("tag", tag, ansi))
        else:
            raise ValueError("unknown tag: <%s>" % tag)
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

```
class AnsiParser:

```
```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
```
```
            ...
```
```
        })
```

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

```
class AnsiParser:
```
```
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
            "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
            "lwhite": "\x1b[97m",
        })
        self._tags.update({
            "fg": "\x1b[38;5;%dm",
            "bg": "\x1b[48;5;%dm",
        })
```

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):

    return "".join(result)
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

```
class AnsiParser:

```
```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
```
```
            ...
```
```
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

```
class AnsiParser:
```
```
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }

            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})

            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
            "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",

            "fg": "\x1b[38;5;%dm",
            "bg": "\x1b[48;5;%dm",

```
```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
```
```
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
            "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
            "lwhite": "\x1b[97m",
        })
        self._tags.update({
            "fg": "\x1b[38;5;%dm",
            "bg": "\x1b[48;5;%dm",
        })
```

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):

    return "".join(result)
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```

But `""` is not None. Hmm.

```
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            "lgreen": "\x1b[92m",
            "lyellow": "\x1b[93m",
            "lblue": "\x1b[94m",
            "lmagenta": "\x1b[95m",
            "lcyan": "\x1b[96m",
            "lwhite": "\x1b[97m",
        })
        self._tags.update({
            "fg": "\x1b[38;5;%dm",
            "bg": "\x1b[48;5;%dm",
        })
```

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Let's search memory from loguru `_colorizer.py`:

```
class AnsiParser:
    def __init__(self):
        self._tokens = []
        self._tag_pattern = re.compile(r"</?([^<>]+)>")
        self._ansi_pattern = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
        self._stack = []
        self._text = []
        self._tags = {}
        self._colors = {
            "black": 30, "red": 31, "green": 32, "yellow": 33, "blue": 34, "magenta": 35, "cyan": 36, "white": 37,
            "light-black": 90, "light-red": 91, "light-green": 92, "light-yellow": 93, "light-blue": 94, "light-magenta": 95, "light-cyan": 96, "light-white": 97,
        }
        self._formats = {
            "bold": 1, "dim": 2, "underline": 4, "blink": 5, "reverse": 7, "strike": 9,
        }
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._colors.items()})
        self._tags.update({name: "\x1b[%dm" % code for name, code in self._formats.items()})
        self._tags.update({
            "normal": "\x1b[0m",
            "reset": "\x1b[0m",
            "clear": "\x1b[0m",
            "lred": "\x1b[91m",
            ...
        })
```

Wait, if `colorize` takes `ansi_level`, maybe it uses it to decide whether to output ANSI codes at all. Let's look at `colorize` method from memory:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    result = []
    for token in tokens:
        if token.type == "text":
            result.append(token.value)
        elif token.type == "tag":
            result.append(token.ansi)
    return "".join(result)
```

But then `ansi_level` is unused. Why pass `""`? Maybe because the method signature in loguru is `colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level=None)` and `ansi_level` is reserved for future? Or maybe `colorize` uses `ansi_level` to determine if it should colorize at all:

```
def colorize(self, tokens, ansi_level):
    if ansi_level is None:
        return self.strip(tokens)
    ...
```
